## Identity and Access Management for Cloud Integration (geändert)
[docs/ISuite/60-Security/identity-and-access-management-for-cloud-integration-f805b22.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite/60-Security/identity-and-access-management-for-cloud-integration-f805b22.md)

```diff
+ -   **Predefined personas** — Each persona is delivered as a ready-to-use role collection \(Cloud Foundry\) or authorization group \(Neo\) covering all the permissions needed for that profile. This is the simpler option and a recommended starting point. For more information, see [Personas](https://help.sap.com/viewer/368c481cd6954bdfa5d0435479fd4eaf/Cloud/en-US/4b4ba1c553474259b5be661f4ef0702c.htm…
+ -   **Individual roles** — For more fine-grained control, you can define your own role collections \(Cloud Foundry\) or authorization groups \(Neo\) by selecting individual roles or role templates that correspond to specific tasks. This approach requires more configuration but lets you tailor permissions precisely to your needs. For more information, see [Tasks and Permissions](tasks-and-permissio…
- Access to dedicated functions of the platform is controlled and protected by authorization checks. A number of authorization groups are available to manage the authorizations of dialog users. An authorization group is based on a persona and defines a set of dedicated permissions relating to the tasks that come into play during the lifecycle of an integration project.
+ Access to dedicated functions of the platform is controlled and protected by authorization checks. Permissions are managed through personas — each representing a typical role in an integration project lifecycle and defining a set of tasks the user is authorized to perform.
+ There are two ways to assign permissions to users:
- > If the logged-in user has to perform tasks such as designing and deploying integration flows, the user must be assigned the authorization group `AuthGroup.IntegrationDeveloper`.
+ > If the logged-in user has to perform tasks such as designing and deploying integration flows, the user must be assigned the authorization group `AuthGroup.IntegrationDeveloper` \(Neo\) or the role collection `PI_Integration_Developer` \(Cloud Foundry\).
```

## Set Up SNC on Edge Integration Cell (geändert)
[docs/ISuite/60-Security/set-up-snc-on-edge-integration-cell-c2315d3.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite/60-Security/set-up-snc-on-edge-integration-cell-c2315d3.md)

```diff
- 2.  To copy the *libsapcrypto.so* file to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp libsapcrypto.so edge-icell/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. Replace the placeholder `<worker-pod>` with the name of a Worker pod. If you have multiple Worker pods, you can select one at random.
+ 2.  To copy the *libsapcrypto.so* file to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp libsapcrypto.so <your-namespace>/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. Replace the placeholder `<worker-pod>` with the name of a Worker pod. If you have multiple Worker pods, you can select one at random.
- 3.  To copy the `sapgenpse` executable file from the same local directory to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp sapgenpse edge-icell/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. The cryptography tool SAPGENPSE is essential for managing the PSE \(Personal Security Environment\) files, which facilitate secure connections.
+ 3.  To copy the `sapgenpse` executable file from the same local directory to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp sapgenpse <your-namespace>/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. The cryptography tool SAPGENPSE is essential for managing the PSE \(Personal Security Environment\) files, which facilitate secure connections.
- 4.  To verify the files have been copied successfully, open a shell session to the Worker pod by running the following command: `kubectl exec -c worker -it -n edge-icell -- /bin/sh`. Execute the following command within the shell session to list the contents of the SNC\_LIB directory: `ls /var/lib/worker/snc/lib`. Ensure the output contains the *libsapcrypto.so* and *sapgenpse* files.
+ 4.  To verify the files have been copied successfully, open a shell session to the Worker pod by running the following command: `kubectl exec -c worker -it -n <your-namespace> -- /bin/sh`. Execute the following command within the shell session to list the contents of the SNC\_LIB directory: `ls /var/lib/worker/snc/lib`. Ensure the output contains the *libsapcrypto.so* and *sapgenpse* files.
+ > ### Note:
+ > The commands and examples in this document use the placeholder *<your-namespace\>*. Replace this with the actual namespace you defined during the deployment. If you kept the default settings, your namespaces are `edge-icell`, `edge-icell-services`, `edge-icell-ela`, or `istio-gateways`.
```

```diff
+ Rules are collected in the following categories:
+ -   **Permission** \(access and usage policies\): Specifies what a consumer is allowed to do, often under certain conditions.
+ -   **Prohibition** \(usage policies only\): Defines what a consumer is not allowed to do, for example, use data within a specific region.
+ -   **Obligation** \(usage policies only\): Defines what a consumer must do, for example, provide data by a certain end date.
- -   If you're creating an **access policy**, add the following parameters:
- Select one of the following attributes to define your rule:
- -   If you're creating a **usage policy**, add the following parameters:
- Select one of the following attributes to define your rule:

+ If you're creating a **usage policy**, add the following parameters to define your rules. You can define rules as **permissions**, **prohibitions**, or **obligations**.
```

## High Availability (geändert)
[docs/ISuite_Edge_Integration_Cell/high-availability-bb56486.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite_Edge_Integration_Cell/high-availability-bb56486.md)

```diff
- Cloud platforms offer multiple availability zones for Kubernetes deployments. For a high availability setup with at least three worker nodes, you need three availability zones to support Multi-AZ with Edge Integration Cell.
+ An availability zone is an isolated failure domain within a data center infrastructure, typically with independent power, cooling, and networking. Cloud platforms provide multiple availability zones out of the box for Kubernetes deployments. You can enable multi-availability zone support on any Kubernetes deployment as long as the underlying infrastructure supports multiple availability zones.
+ For a high availability setup with Edge Integration Cell, you need at least three worker nodes distributed across three availability zones.
```

```diff
- 2.  To copy the *libsapcrypto.so* file to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp libsapcrypto.so edge-icell/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. Replace the placeholder `<worker-pod>` with the name of a Worker pod. If you have multiple Worker pods, you can select one at random.
+ 2.  To copy the *libsapcrypto.so* file to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp libsapcrypto.so <your-namespace>/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. Replace the placeholder `<worker-pod>` with the name of a Worker pod. If you have multiple Worker pods, you can select one at random.
- 3.  To copy the `sapgenpse` executable file from the same local directory to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp sapgenpse edge-icell/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. The cryptography tool SAPGENPSE is essential for managing the PSE \(Personal Security Environment\) files, which facilitate secure connections.
+ 3.  To copy the `sapgenpse` executable file from the same local directory to the Worker pod, run the following command: `kubectl cp sapgenpse <your-namespace>/<worker-pod>:/var/lib/worker/snc/lib -c worker`. The cryptography tool SAPGENPSE is essential for managing the PSE \(Personal Security Environment\) files, which facilitate secure connections.
- 4.  To verify the files have been copied successfully, open a shell session to the Worker pod by running the following command: `kubectl exec -c worker -it -n edge-icell -- /bin/sh`. Execute the following command within the shell session to list the contents of the SNC\_LIB directory: `ls /var/lib/worker/snc/lib`. Ensure the output contains the *libsapcrypto.so* and *sapgenpse* files.
+ 4.  To verify the files have been copied successfully, open a shell session to the Worker pod by running the following command: `kubectl exec -c worker -it -n <your-namespace> -- /bin/sh`. Execute the following command within the shell session to list the contents of the SNC\_LIB directory: `ls /var/lib/worker/snc/lib`. Ensure the output contains the *libsapcrypto.so* and *sapgenpse* files.
+ > ### Note:
+ > The commands and examples in this document use the placeholder *<your-namespace\>*. Replace this with the actual namespace you defined during the deployment. If you kept the default settings, your namespaces are `edge-icell`, `edge-icell-services`, `edge-icell-ela`, or `istio-gateways`.
```

## Overview Of B2B Standards (geändert)
[docs/ISuite_Integration_Advisor/overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite_Integration_Advisor/overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md)

```diff
+ -   [RosettaNet](overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md#loioc339707982d141efa7c7849d033ca70d__section_rosettanet)
+ <a name="loioc339707982d141efa7c7849d033ca70d__section_rosettanet"/>
+ The RosettaNet message standard used a different versioning strategy than other standards \(for example, [UN/EDIFACT](overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md#loioc339707982d141efa7c7849d033ca70d__section_h1c_cvx_qsb)\), as it didn't publish full release versions. RosettaNet versioned all Partner Interface Processes \(PIPs\) separately.
+ Integration Advisor currently offers all RosettaNet PIPs of the series `01`, `02`, and `03` with either the status `V` \(Validated\) or the final status `R` \(Released\).
+ **Message Types \(269\)**
+ PIP0A1\_V02\_00\_FailureNotification
+ 0A1\_MS\_V02\_00\_FailureNotification.dtd
+ PIP0C1\_R01\_01\_AsynchronousTestNotification
+ 0C1\_MS\_R01\_01\_AsynchronousTestNotification.dtd
+ PIP0C1\_R01\_02\_AsynchronousTestNotification
+ 0C1\_MS\_R01\_02\_AsynchronousTestNotification.dtd
+ PIP0C2\_R01\_01\_AsynchronousTestConfirmation
```

## Identity and Access Management for Cloud Integration (geändert)
[docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/60-Security/identity-and-access-management-for-cloud-integration-f805b22.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/60-Security/identity-and-access-management-for-cloud-integration-f805b22.md)

```diff
+ -   **Predefined personas** — Each persona is delivered as a ready-to-use role collection \(Cloud Foundry\) or authorization group \(Neo\) covering all the permissions needed for that profile. This is the simpler option and a recommended starting point. For more information, see [Personas](https://help.sap.com/viewer/368c481cd6954bdfa5d0435479fd4eaf/Cloud/en-US/4b4ba1c553474259b5be661f4ef0702c.htm…
+ -   **Individual roles** — For more fine-grained control, you can define your own role collections \(Cloud Foundry\) or authorization groups \(Neo\) by selecting individual roles or role templates that correspond to specific tasks. This approach requires more configuration but lets you tailor permissions precisely to your needs. For more information, see [Tasks and Permissions](tasks-and-permissio…
- Access to dedicated functions of the platform is controlled and protected by authorization checks. A number of authorization groups are available to manage the authorizations of dialog users. An authorization group is based on a persona and defines a set of dedicated permissions relating to the tasks that come into play during the lifecycle of an integration project.
+ Access to dedicated functions of the platform is controlled and protected by authorization checks. Permissions are managed through personas — each representing a typical role in an integration project lifecycle and defining a set of tasks the user is authorized to perform.
+ There are two ways to assign permissions to users:
- > If the logged-in user has to perform tasks such as designing and deploying integration flows, the user must be assigned the authorization group `AuthGroup.IntegrationDeveloper`.
+ > If the logged-in user has to perform tasks such as designing and deploying integration flows, the user must be assigned the authorization group `AuthGroup.IntegrationDeveloper` \(Neo\) or the role collection `PI_Integration_Developer` \(Cloud Foundry\).
```

```diff
+ -   **Acknowledgement \(ERP\) support**: The adapter can request signed ERP messages. When it receives an ERP \(EERP or NERP\), the adapter tries to correlate them to processed messages or files and adds them to an `ERPDeliveryReportsPackage` XML in the response. See: [Synchronous ERP Delivery Report](configure-the-oftp-receiver-adapter-8a6b283.md#loio8a6b283dade6419eb907920117983156__erp).
- -   **Dynamic Configuration**: Provides dynamic configuration. You can override most fields for each message through headers or exchange properties. See [Dynamic Configuration and Per-message Overrides](configure-the-oftp-receiver-adapter-8a6b283.md#loio8a6b283dade6419eb907920117983156__dynamic).
+ -   **Dynamic Configuration**: Provides dynamic configuration. You can override most fields for each message through headers or exchange properties. ## Message Processing Log - Adapter Tracing (geändert)
```
[docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/message-processing-log-adapter-tracing-a9db4ea.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/message-processing-log-adapter-tracing-a9db4ea.md)

```diff
- SAP Cloud Integration adopts the W3C Trace Context standard as the universal trace identifier for cross-system tracing. W3C Trace Context is an open web standard defined by the World Wide Web Consortium \(W3C\). Cloud Integration includes the W3C Trace ID inside the SAP Passport so that the correlation chain is preserved when a message crosses from an OpenTelemetry-instrumented system into the SAP…
+ Cloud Integration adopts the W3C Trace Context standard as the universal trace identifier for cross-system tracing. W3C Trace Context is an open web standard defined by the World Wide Web Consortium \(W3C\). SAPCloud Integration includes the W3C Trace ID inside the SAP Passport so that the correlation chain is preserved when a message crosses from an OpenTelemetry-instrumented system into the SAP …
+ ### W3C Trace Context Propagation Scenarios
+ Cloud Integration evaluates inbound requests for the presence of an SAP Passport and a W3C Trace Context header \(`traceparent`\) and applies the following rules to determine how trace context is propagated. Regardless of the inbound scenario, both the `sap-passport` and `traceparent` headers are included in outbound requests by default.
- > For SAP-to-SAP communication, the SAP Passport remains the primary carrier of trace information. Cloud Integration includes the W3C Trace ID inside the SAP Passport so that the correlation chain is preserved when a message crosses from an OpenTelemetry-instrumented system into the SAP ecosystem.
+ > The outbound `traceparent` is derived from the SAP Passport, with the W3C Trace ID corresponding to the SAP Passport Transaction ID.
+ If no SAP Passport is present in the integration flow, an SAP Passport is generated from the inbound `traceparent` header. The W3C Trace ID maps to the SAP Passport Transaction ID.
+ > You can prevent the propagation of the `traceparent` header in outbound requests by setting the `SAP_DisableExternalW3CTraceContextTransmission` exchange property to `true` in the *Content Modifier* step of the integration flow. This property controls only the automatic derivation of `traceparent` from the SAP Passport. A `traceparent` header explicitly added in the *Content Modifier* step is no…
+ > ### Note:
+ > For SAP-to-SAP communication, the SAP Passport remains the primary carrier of trace information.
+ </td>
+ </tr>
```

## Optimize Performance (geändert)
[docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/optimize-performance-491c80d.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/optimize-performance-491c80d.md)

```diff
- An incoming 1-KB message is processed faster and with less resources than a 5-MB message \(for the same integration flow design\). In particular, if your integration scenario uses many message transformation steps, make sure to process only messages that don't exceed the following size:
+ Message size is one of the most direct factors affecting both performance and memory consumption. Example, an incoming 1-KB message is processed faster and with fewer resources than a 5-MB message for the same integration flow design.
- -   Payload size: 40 MB
- -   Aggregated size for attachments: 100 MB
- SAP is doing the general capacity management of all your SAP Integration Suite tenants, either of productively used tenants or test tenants. Capacity management is also based on the license model. Therefore, you don't need to care about that. However, memory is a limited resource in the Java virtual machines. Before you use your integration flows productively, it's recommended to perform extensive…
+ There is no fixed maximum message size, efficient processing depends on following factors like:
+ -   Integration flow design
+ -   Number and type of processing steps, adapters in use
+ -   Frequency of concurrent messages
+ -   Tenant environment and sizing
+ Resource-efficient flows on appropriately sized tenants can process significantly larger messages.
- Following this recommendation helps you to avoid memory bottlenecks. Note that, however, there's no general maximum message size. If the integration flow is designed resource-efficient, it can process way larger messages.
```

## Where Used (geändert)
[docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/where-used-0559942.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ISuite_Integrations_APIs/where-used-0559942.md)

```diff
- This feature lets you identify all integration flows that reference a specific security material.
+ This feature lets you identify all integration flows that reference a specific security material through externalized parameters.
- ## Scope and Static References
+ ## Scope and Externalized References
- Where Used identifies security materials that are statically referenced in the design-time integration flow configuration. Security materials referenced dynamically, for example, through message headers or exchange properties at runtime are not detected.
+ Where Used identifies security materials that are referenced through externalized parameters in the design-time integration flow configuration. Security materials that are statically referenced \(hardcoded directly in the flow configuration\), or referenced dynamically through message headers or exchange properties at runtime, are not detected.
```

## OData Provisioning (geändert)

```diff
- OData Provisioning, a capability of SAP Integration Suite, exposes business data and business logic as OData services on SAP Business Technology Platform, enabling you to run user-centric applications.
+ OData Provisioning, a capability of SAP Integration Suite, exposes business data and business logic as OData services on SAP BTP, enabling you to run user-centric applications.
- This capability streamlines the way in which SAP Gateway OData services are exposed to SAP Business Technology Platform. It replaces the need for an SAP Gateway hub \(front-end server\) in the cloud and reduces the total cost of ownership and shifts the responsibility for maintenance and security tasks from you to the cloud provider.
+ This capability streamlines the way in which SAP Gateway OData services are exposed to SAP BTP. It replaces the need for an SAP Gateway hub \(front-end server\) in the cloud and reduces the total cost of ownership and shifts the responsibility for maintenance and security tasks from you to the cloud provider.
```

```diff
+ 2026-10-18
+ 2026-10-18
+ 2609
+ 2026-10-18
+ 2026-10-18
+ 2609
+ Support for MCP Specification 2026-07-28
+ SAP Integration Suite now supports Model Context Protocol \(MCP\) specification version 2026-07-28, in addition to the existing support for version 2025-11-25. The 2026-07-28 specification introduces a stateless, per-request interaction model, with changes to protocol negotiation, HTTP header handling, result handling, and capability negotiation.
+ 2026-10-18
+ 2026-10-18
+ 2609
+ Client SDK version 3.0.6
```

```diff
- *Principal Propagation* – only if the *Proxy Type* is *On-premise*.The tenant authenticates itself against the receiver by forwarding the principal of the inbound user to the cloud connector, and from there to the back end of the relevant on-premise system.
- Follow the steps to configure technical user in Cloud Foundry:
- -   Configuration in the Cloud Connector - Cloud Connector should read the technical user principal and add it as Common Name \(CN\) in the certificate that is generated by Cloud Connector. To read the technical user principal there are two configuration options:
- For example: In the *On-premise* system if you have mapped a string MIKE to a business user BSR\_USA, then in Cloud Connector in the condition, provide the pattern $\{client\_id\} and in the value provide the client id generated in CPI subaccount, In the subject pattern provide the string MIKE.
- -   Map the Client ID to the actual user in the *On-premise* system.
```

## Optimize Performance (geändert)
[docs/ci/Development/optimize-performance-491c80d.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ci/Development/optimize-performance-491c80d.md)

```diff
- An incoming 1-KB message is processed faster and with less resources than a 5-MB message \(for the same integration flow design\). In particular, if your integration scenario uses many message transformation steps, make sure to process only messages that don't exceed the following size:
+ Message size is one of the most direct factors affecting both performance and memory consumption. Example, an incoming 1-KB message is processed faster and with fewer resources than a 5-MB message for the same integration flow design.
- -   Payload size: 40 MB
- -   Aggregated size for attachments: 100 MB
- SAP is doing the general capacity management of all your SAP Cloud Integration tenants, either of productively used tenants or test tenants. Capacity management is also based on the license model. Therefore, you don't need to care about that. However, memory is a limited resource in the Java virtual machines. Before you use your integration flows productively, it's recommended to perform extensive…
+ There is no fixed maximum message size, efficient processing depends on following factors like:
+ -   Integration flow design
+ -   Number and type of processing steps, adapters in use
+ -   Frequency of concurrent messages
+ -   Tenant environment and sizing
+ Resource-efficient flows on appropriately sized tenants can process significantly larger messages.
- Following this recommendation helps you to avoid memory bottlenecks. Note that, however, there's no general maximum message size. If the integration flow is designed resource-efficient, it can process way larger messages.
```

## Overview Of B2B Standards (geändert)
[docs/ci/IntegrationAdvisor/overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ci/IntegrationAdvisor/overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md)

```diff
+ -   [RosettaNet](overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md#loioc339707982d141efa7c7849d033ca70d__section_rosettanet)
+ <a name="loioc339707982d141efa7c7849d033ca70d__section_rosettanet"/>
+ The RosettaNet message standard used a different versioning strategy than other standards \(for example, [UN/EDIFACT](overview-of-b2b-standards-c339707.md#loioc339707982d141efa7c7849d033ca70d__section_h1c_cvx_qsb)\), as it didn't publish full release versions. RosettaNet versioned all Partner Interface Processes \(PIPs\) separately.
+ Integration Advisor currently offers all RosettaNet PIPs of the series `01`, `02`, and `03` with either the status `V` \(Validated\) or the final status `R` \(Released\).
+ **Message Types \(269\)**
+ PIP0A1\_V02\_00\_FailureNotification
+ 0A1\_MS\_V02\_00\_FailureNotification.dtd
+ PIP0C1\_R01\_01\_AsynchronousTestNotification
+ 0C1\_MS\_R01\_01\_AsynchronousTestNotification.dtd
+ PIP0C1\_R01\_02\_AsynchronousTestNotification
+ 0C1\_MS\_R01\_02\_AsynchronousTestNotification.dtd
+ PIP0C2\_R01\_01\_AsynchronousTestConfirmation
```

## Message Processing Log - Adapter Tracing (geändert)
[docs/ci/Operations/message-processing-log-adapter-tracing-a9db4ea.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ci/Operations/message-processing-log-adapter-tracing-a9db4ea.md)

```diff
- SAP Cloud Integration adopts the W3C Trace Context standard as the universal trace identifier for cross-system tracing. W3C Trace Context is an open web standard defined by the World Wide Web Consortium \(W3C\). Cloud Integration includes the W3C Trace ID inside the SAP Passport so that the correlation chain is preserved when a message crosses from an OpenTelemetry-instrumented system into the SAP…
+ adopts the W3C Trace Context standard as the universal trace identifier for cross-system tracing. W3C Trace Context is an open web standard defined by the World Wide Web Consortium \(W3C\). SAPCloud Integration includes the W3C Trace ID inside the SAP Passport so that the correlation chain is preserved when a message crosses from an OpenTelemetry-instrumented system into the SAP ecosystem.
- If the incoming request includes a W3C trace context header \(`traceparent`\), Cloud Integration captures the trace parent in the message processing log. This allows you to correlate the MPL entry with the corresponding record in an external monitoring tool.
+ If the incoming request includes a W3C trace context header \(`traceparent`\), captures the trace parent in the message processing log. This allows you to correlate the MPL entry with the corresponding record in an external monitoring tool.
+ ### W3C Trace Context Propagation Scenarios
+ evaluates inbound requests for the presence of an SAP Passport and a W3C Trace Context header \(`traceparent`\) and applies the following rules to determine how trace context is propagated. Regardless of the inbound scenario, both the `sap-passport` and `traceparent` headers are included in outbound requests by default.
- > For SAP-to-SAP communication, the SAP Passport remains the primary carrier of trace information. Cloud Integration includes the W3C Trace ID inside the SAP Passport so that the correlation chain is preserved when a message crosses from an OpenTelemetry-instrumented system into the SAP ecosystem.
+ > The outbound `traceparent` is derived from the SAP Passport, with the W3C Trace ID corresponding to the SAP Passport Transaction ID.
+ If no SAP Passport is present in the integration flow, an SAP Passport is generated from the inbound `traceparent` header. The W3C Trace ID maps to the SAP Passport Transaction ID.
+ > You can prevent the propagation of the `traceparent` header in outbound requests by setting the `SAP_DisableExternalW3CTraceContextTransmission` exchange property to `true` in the *Content Modifier* step of the integration flow. This property controls only the automatic derivation of `traceparent` from the SAP Passport. A `traceparent` header explicitly added in the *Content Modifier* step is no…
+ > ### Note:
+ > For SAP-to-SAP communication, the SAP Passport remains the primary carrier of trace information.
```

## Identity and Access Management (geändert)
[docs/ci/SecurityCF/identity-and-access-management-f805b22.md](https://github.com/SAP-docs/btp-integration-suite/blob/147acb7d15af1e4fb86e3093cc3e11d4782d9043/docs/ci/SecurityCF/identity-and-access-management-f805b22.md)

```diff
+ -   **Predefined personas** — Each persona is delivered as a ready-to-use role collection \(Cloud Foundry\) or authorization group \(Neo\) covering all the permissions needed for that profile. This is the simpler option and a recommended starting point. For more information, see [Personas](personas-4b4ba1c.md).
+ -   **Individual roles** — For more fine-grained control, you can define your own role collections \(Cloud Foundry\) or authorization groups \(Neo\) by selecting individual roles or role templates that correspond to specific tasks. This approach requires more configuration but lets you tailor permissions precisely to your needs. For more information, see [Tasks and Permissions](../SecurityNeo/task…
- Access to dedicated functions of the platform is controlled and protected by authorization checks. A number of authorization groups are available to manage the authorizations of dialog users. An authorization group is based on a persona and defines a set of dedicated permissions relating to the tasks that come into play during the lifecycle of an integration project.
+ Access to dedicated functions of the platform is controlled and protected by authorization checks. Permissions are managed through personas — each representing a typical role in an integration project lifecycle and defining a set of tasks the user is authorized to perform.
+ There are two ways to assign permissions to users:
- > If the logged-in user has to perform tasks such as designing and deploying integration flows, the user must be assigned the authorization group `AuthGroup.IntegrationDeveloper`.
+ > If the logged-in user has to perform tasks such as designing and deploying integration flows, the user must be assigned the authorization group `AuthGroup.IntegrationDeveloper` \(Neo\) or the role collection `PI_Integration_Developer` \(Cloud Foundry\).
```

```diff
+ ## October 2026
+ Software Increment: 2608
+ 7.56.22
+ com.sybase.jdbc4.jdbc.SybSQLException: Invalid column name
+ 8.47.24
+ 6.83.112
+ 6.83.108
+ 8.47.22
+ -   Fixes an issue where JSON key ordering could silently break on Java 21 for Groovy scripts using `JsonSlurper.parseText()`.
+ -   After the recent parallel deployment enhancement, OData Sender endpoint registration became inconsistent on certain worker nodes, causing 404 responses on invocation. This was due to a race condition introduced by concurrent deployment threads during endpoint registration. The fix ensures that endpoint registration is handled correctly in the parallel, multi-threaded deployment flow.
+ 6.83.105
+ -   Free usage of the AI feature has been extended from September 30 to January 2027.
```
